Your final correction is good: I verified commit `7d2f0c9`, and the reward-preview close/upgrade loophole is closed. Closing the modal now clears the pending callback instead of granting it, and tapping Upgrade opens the honest “Coming Soon” pass screen rather than setting `isProUser` to true.[^10_1]

For a true native Android app without installing VS Code or Android Studio, use **GitHub Codespaces in your browser as your code editor** and **Expo on your Samsung phone** as the native preview environment. Use Google AI Studio as your planning/code-generation helper, but do not use it as the place where the native project actually lives or runs.

## Your tool setup

Use these three things:


| Tool | What you use it for | Do you need to install it? |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| GitHub Codespaces | Browser-based code editor and terminal; this replaces VS Code on your computer | No |
| Expo Go on Samsung | Opens the native React Native development app on your physical phone | Yes, install from Google Play |
| Google AI Studio | Helps generate or revise small pieces of React Native code | No computer installation |

GitHub Codespaces can be created directly from a repository in the browser through **Code → Codespaces → Create codespace**. It gives you a browser version of the editor and an integrated terminal.[^10_2]

Expo’s official `create-expo-app` tool creates a React Native/Expo project; Expo Go can open the running native development project from the QR code shown in the terminal.[^10_3][^10_4][^10_5]

## Step 1: Keep alarmG2 unchanged

Your current repository is now your **web prototype and product blueprint**:

```text
rainbowblurry-book/alarmG2
```

Do not delete it. Do not turn it into an Expo project. Do not add Android files, Expo packages, or React Native code to it.

It contains the important product decisions you worked hard on:

- Alarm flow and two-stage snooze behavior.
- Barcode dismissal rules.
- Movement mission rules.
- Dark visual identity.
- Theme palette and screen layout.
- Copy/text for the user flow.
- Honest “Coming Soon” monetization UI.

The current commit history includes the essential cleanup and the final reward loophole fix, so you have a stable reference point.[^10_1]

## Step 2: Create the native repository

On the GitHub website, create a completely new repository:

```text
Repository name: alarmG-native
Visibility: Private
Initialize repository: Leave README, .gitignore, and license unchecked
```

Leaving it empty makes the Expo creation step much easier.

You can use `main` only. Since you are working alone and GitHub authentication is confusing right now, that is okay. The important rule is simply:

```text
alarmG2 = do not change; web prototype
alarmG-native = native Android development
```


## Step 3: Open browser editor

Open your new `alarmG-native` GitHub repository.

1. Click the green **Code** button.
2. Select the **Codespaces** tab.
3. Click **Create codespace on main**.
4. Wait for the browser editor to load.
5. When it opens, look at the bottom panel and select **Terminal**.

You now have a full development environment in your browser. You do not need to install VS Code locally.

## Step 4: Create Expo project

In the Codespaces terminal, paste this command exactly:

```bash
npx create-expo-app@latest . --template blank-typescript
```

Important: the final dot `.` means:

```text
Create the Expo app inside this current empty alarmG-native repository.
```

When it asks for confirmation, choose the default option or type:

```text
y
```

When the command finishes, you should see files similar to:

```text
App.tsx
app.json
package.json
tsconfig.json
assets/
node_modules/
```

This is your new true React Native project. It is not a Vite project, not a webpage, not a Capacitor wrapper, and not a PWA. Expo is designed to build one JavaScript/TypeScript project into native mobile apps.[^10_6][^10_3]

## Step 5: Save the clean starter

Before changing anything, commit the fresh Expo starter project.

In the Codespaces terminal, run these commands one at a time:

```bash
git status
```

Then:

```bash
git add .
```

Then:

```bash
git commit -m "Initialize native Expo TypeScript app"
```

Then:

```bash
git push origin main
```

If GitHub asks you to authenticate, complete the GitHub browser authorization screen. Do not worry if it feels unfamiliar—Codespaces normally uses your signed-in GitHub account, so this is generally simpler than local Git authentication.

After the push, open the `alarmG-native` repository page in another browser tab and confirm you can see the commit:

```text
Initialize native Expo TypeScript app
```


## Step 6: Open it on Samsung

On your Samsung phone:

1. Install **Expo Go** from Google Play if you do not already have it.
2. Return to the Codespaces browser tab.
3. In the terminal, run:
```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

Wait until you see a QR code.

4. Open **Expo Go** on your Samsung phone.
5. Select **Scan QR code**.
6. Scan the QR code in Codespaces.
7. Wait for the starter app to open.

The first screen will probably look plain and generic. That is correct. At this stage you are only proving that:

```text
Codespaces → Expo project → Samsung phone
```

works before we add your actual alarm UI.

Expo Go is for the early native-development stage. Later, when we add custom Android alarm scheduling, a native development build will replace Expo Go because background alarm features require custom native Android code.[^10_5][^10_7]

### Important terminal rule

While this command is running:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

the terminal is busy.

When you need to type another terminal command later, press:

```text
Ctrl + C
```

in the Codespaces terminal to stop the Expo server first. Then run your command. Afterward, start it again with:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```


## Step 7: Do not add features yet

At this point, do **not** ask Google AI Studio to migrate all features.

Do not add:

```text
AlarmManager
Camera
Barcode scanner
Notifications
Background scheduling
Exact alarm permissions
Audio system
Motion sensors
Payments
Ads
Themes
Custom file uploads
```

You are building a foundation in the correct order.

The very first native milestone is only this:

```text
A native app that opens on your Samsung phone
and has three bottom tabs:

Alarm
Setup
Settings
```

No real alarm behavior yet. No scanner yet. No monetization yet.

## Step 8: Use Google AI Studio correctly

Once the Expo starter opens successfully on your phone, go back to Google AI Studio and paste this prompt:

```text
I have created a new, blank Expo React Native TypeScript project.

This is a true native mobile project.
It is not a Vite app.
It is not a React web app.
It is not a Capacitor wrapper.
It must not use div, span, button, input, HTML, CSS files, browser APIs, react-dom, or Tailwind web classes.

I will copy the code manually into GitHub Codespaces.

Generate only the first native UI milestone.

Goal:
Create a dark React Native app with three native bottom tabs:
1. Alarm
2. Setup
3. Settings

Requirements:
- Use React Native components only:
  View, Text, Pressable, SafeAreaView, ScrollView, StyleSheet.
- Use TypeScript.
- Use React Navigation bottom tabs.
- Use a dark Samsung-inspired visual style.
- Keep the visual mood close to my existing WakeUp web prototype:
  dark obsidian background, blue accent, amber alarm action, rounded cards, high-contrast white text.
- Create static UI only.
- Do not add functional alarm scheduling.
- Do not add camera, barcode scanning, notifications, haptics, audio, motion sensors, custom file upload, login, cloud database, payments, ads, promo codes, founder controls, or native Android modules.
- Do not include web code or browser APIs.
- Do not create app.json changes yet.

Return only:
1. Packages I need to install
2. Exact terminal install command
3. Exact file tree
4. Full copy-paste code for every new or replaced file
5. Exact command to run the native app afterward
```

Do not blindly paste its output yet. First bring its response back here, and I will check that it is actually valid React Native/Expo code and that it is not secretly generating another web-style project.

## Your immediate checklist

Do these things in order:

```text
1. Create the empty private GitHub repository: alarmG-native.
2. Create a Codespace on its main branch.
3. Run:
   npx create-expo-app@latest . --template blank-typescript
4. Commit and push:
   Initialize native Expo TypeScript app
5. Install/open Expo Go on Samsung.
6. Run:
   npx expo start --tunnel
7. Scan the QR code in Expo Go.
8. Confirm the starter app opens on the phone.
9. Send me the Google AI Studio native-UI response before pasting its code.
```

At that point, I will guide you through the first three-tab native interface without overwhelming the project or destroying the clean app foundation.

## Fix the README block

You are already in the correct Codespaces folder:

```text
/workspaces/alarmG-native
```

In the terminal, run this exact command:

```bash
rm README.md
```

This removes only the starter README that is blocking Expo from writing into the repository root.

Then immediately run:

```bash
npx create-expo-app@latest . --template blank-typescript
```

When it asks:

```text
Ok to proceed? (y)
```

type:

```text
y
```

and press Enter.

## What should happen

This time, Expo should create the native project in the current repository. At the end, you should see a file structure similar to:

```text
App.tsx
app.json
assets/
package.json
tsconfig.json
node_modules/
```

Do not worry if the terminal shows many package-install lines. Wait until the command fully finishes and gives you the normal shell prompt again:

```text
@rainbowblurry-book ➜ /workspaces/alarmG-native (main) $
```


## Confirm project creation

After the Expo command finishes, run:

```bash
ls
```

You should see at least:

```text
App.tsx
app.json
assets
package.json
tsconfig.json
```

Then run:

```bash
git status
```

This time, you should see new untracked files or files ready to be added.

## Save the native starter

Only after you see those Expo files, run these commands one at a time:

```bash
git add .
```

```bash
git commit -m "Initialize native Expo TypeScript app"
```

```bash
git push origin main
```

Do not run `npx expo start --tunnel` yet. First, let the starter project finish generating and let the initial commit reach GitHub.

## If it fails again

If `create-expo-app` still says that files might be overwritten, do not delete anything else randomly. Run:

```bash
ls -la
```

Then copy the complete output here. I will tell you exactly which file is safe to remove or what command to use next.

## Open it on Samsung

### 1. Install Expo Go

On your Samsung phone:

1. Open Google Play Store.
2. Search for:

```text
Expo Go
```

3. Install the official Expo Go app.
4. Open it once and allow camera access if it asks; it needs this only to scan the development QR code.

### 2. Start the native app

Back in GitHub Codespaces, make sure your terminal prompt looks like this:

```text
@rainbowblurry-book ➜ /workspaces/alarmG-native (main) $
```

Then run:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

Wait. The terminal should eventually show a large QR code and a line similar to:

```text
Metro waiting on exp://...
```

If it asks whether it can install or use a tunnel/Ngrok dependency, choose:

```text
y
```


### 3. Scan the QR code

On your Samsung phone:

1. Open **Expo Go**.
2. Tap **Scan QR code**.
3. Scan the QR code visible in the Codespaces terminal.
4. Wait for the app to load.

You should see a simple default Expo starter screen. That screen will not look like WakeUp yet—and that is exactly fine.

At this moment, you are proving this native development chain works:

```text
GitHub repository
→ GitHub Codespaces browser editor
→ Expo development server
→ Expo Go
→ Samsung phone
```


## If the phone cannot connect

If Expo Go shows a connection error, copy the exact terminal output here. Do not switch randomly between commands.

The first thing I will check is whether Codespaces successfully created the tunnel. The correct first command remains:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

Do not use this for now:

```bash
npm run web
```

That would open a browser-oriented version, which is not what you want.

## After it opens

Once the blank Expo starter appears on your Samsung, stop the Expo server before typing another command:

```text
Ctrl + C
```

Then send Google AI Studio the following prompt:

5\. One exact command to start the app on my Samsung phone
codeBash
npx expo start --android

The AI Studio response is a usable **first native UI shell**: it is React Native code rather than web/Vite code, and it deliberately avoids alarms, camera, payments, and Android background logic. Do apply this stage—but make three small corrections first: use Expo-compatible installation commands, start the app with a tunnel rather than `--android`, and change the hard-coded “30 steps” copy.[^13_1]

Your `alarmG-native` repository is correctly initialized as a native Expo TypeScript project, with the base app configuration, assets, and entry files committed in `b95f1b8`.[^13_1]

## Before you paste code

### Do not use this command

Do **not** run the command AI Studio suggested:

```bash
npx expo start --android
```

In GitHub Codespaces, that command tries to launch an Android emulator or connect through Android Debug Bridge. Codespaces does not have a local Android emulator connected to your Samsung phone.

Use this later instead:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

That gives you a QR code that Expo Go on your Samsung phone can open. Expo’s standard device-development workflow uses the QR code produced by the development server.[^13_2][^13_3]

### Install navigation correctly

In the Codespaces terminal, run these **two** commands one at a time:

```bash
npx expo install @react-navigation/native react-native-screens react-native-safe-area-context
```

```bash
npm install @react-navigation/bottom-tabs
```

Use `npx expo install` for React Native packages that must match your Expo SDK version. It selects compatible native dependency versions rather than leaving npm to choose arbitrary versions. React Navigation’s bottom-tabs package is then installed separately.[^13_4][^13_5]

Do **not** separately install this:

```bash
@expo/vector-icons
```

Your Expo project already includes access to Expo icon support. Also, Expo’s current documentation marks `@expo/vector-icons` as deprecated/not recommended for new long-term work, but for this small first shell, the supplied `Ionicons` import is acceptable and will keep the setup simple.[^13_6][^13_7]

After the two installation commands finish, run:

```bash
git status
```

You should see changes to:

```text
package.json
package-lock.json
```

Do not commit yet. First add the native UI files.

## Apply the native UI

### Create folders

In the left-side **Explorer** panel in Codespaces:

1. Right-click the empty space beneath the root files.
2. Select **New Folder**.
3. Create this folder:

```text
src
```

4. Inside `src`, create these folders:

```text
navigation
screens
theme
```


Your file structure should become:

```text
alarmG-native/
├── App.tsx
├── app.json
├── index.ts
├── package.json
├── assets/
└── src/
    ├── navigation/
    │   └── AppNavigator.tsx
    ├── screens/
    │   ├── AlarmScreen.tsx
    │   ├── SetupScreen.tsx
    │   └── SettingsScreen.tsx
    └── theme/
        └── colors.ts
```


### Create and replace files

Use the code from AI Studio exactly for these five files:

```text
src/theme/colors.ts
src/navigation/AppNavigator.tsx
src/screens/AlarmScreen.tsx
src/screens/SetupScreen.tsx
src/screens/SettingsScreen.tsx
```

Then replace the entire existing root `App.tsx` with AI Studio’s `App.tsx` code.

In Codespaces:

1. Click a file in the Explorer.
2. Press:

```text
Ctrl + A
```

3. Paste the full code supplied by AI Studio.
4. Press:

```text
Ctrl + S
```

5. Repeat for each file.

### Make these two text edits

In `src/screens/SetupScreen.tsx`, find:

```tsx
<Text style={styles.cardDescription}>
  Walk 30 steps away from your bed to dismiss the alarm.
</Text>
```

Replace it with:

```tsx
<Text style={styles.cardDescription}>
  Complete your movement target before scanning to dismiss the alarm.
</Text>
```

Your earlier project used a configurable movement target with a default of 15, so “30 steps” would create an unnecessary conflict between the native UI and your actual future settings model. The static screen should describe the behavior without hard-coding a number yet.

In `src/screens/AlarmScreen.tsx`, find:

```tsx
<Text style={styles.cardSubtitle}>
  Your alarm is set for early morning
</Text>
```

Replace it with:

```tsx
<Text style={styles.cardSubtitle}>
  Set a wake time and select the days you need it.
</Text>
```

At this stage, `06:30` is a static visual placeholder, not a working alarm setting. This wording is accurate and avoids making the user think the native feature already works.

## Run it on your Samsung

After all six files are saved, run this in the terminal:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

Wait for the QR code.

On your Samsung:

1. Open **Expo Go**.
2. Tap **Scan QR code**.
3. Scan the terminal QR code.
4. Wait for the app to open.

You should see:

- A dark native bottom tab bar.
- Three tabs: Alarm, Setup, Settings.
- A large `06:30 AM` clock on Alarm.
- Weekday chips.
- An amber Arm Alarm button.
- Dark rounded cards on all screens.
- Static mission and settings content.

This is the first true native screen milestone. It is not a browser page packaged in an app: React Native renders native interface primitives through Expo.[^13_8][^13_9]

## If you see an error

Do not make random changes. Copy the **full red Expo error message** or the complete Codespaces terminal output here.

The most likely early errors would be:


| Error | Meaning | What to send me |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `Unable to resolve @react-navigation/...` | A package command did not finish or a package name is missing | Terminal output |
| `Cannot find module ../screens/...` | A folder/file name differs from the import | File tree screenshot or copied Explorer names |
| `Unable to resolve @expo/vector-icons` | Expo dependency resolution needs adjustment | Exact red error text |
| QR code works but phone cannot load app | Tunnel/session issue | Full `npx expo start --tunnel` output |

## Commit only after it opens

Once the three-tab app opens successfully on your Samsung, stop Expo with:

```text
Ctrl + C
```

Then commit your native UI shell:

```bash
git add .
```

```bash
git commit -m "Build native three-tab WakeUp UI shell"
```

```bash
git push origin main
```

Do **not** add alarm scheduling, notifications, scanner functionality, motion sensing, audio, payments, ads, or native Android permissions in this commit. Keep this commit focused: it proves the design is now truly native and that your browser-based Codespaces → Expo Go → Samsung workflow works.

After it is pushed, send me:

```text
1. The new commit ID
2. Whether all three tabs opened on Samsung
3. Any visual issue you noticed
```

Then we will start the next small stage: turn the **Alarm** screen from static UI into a real editable wake-time and weekday-selection screen, still without adding background alarm scheduling yet.

The native app is very close to opening. The actual blocker is just one missing dependency: the generated navigation code imports `@expo/vector-icons`, but the blank Expo template did not include that package.

I gave you incorrect guidance earlier when I said not to install it. For this specific code, you **do need to install it**. The React Native DevTools `libatk-1.0.so.0` message is a GitHub Codespaces/Linux environment warning and is not what stopped your app; Metro correctly continued and identified the real missing package.[^14_1]

Your Expo terminal is currently running. First stop it:

```text

```

```text
@rainbowblurry-book ➜ /workspaces/alarmG-native (main) $
```

```text
Unable to resolve "@expo/vector-icons"
```

You should see the native three-tab shell:

```text
Alarm | Setup | Settings
```

- Dark background.

- Alarm tab showing `06:30 AM`.
- Weekday chips.
- Amber **Arm Alarm** button.
- Setup cards for wake-up item, barcode mission, and movement mission.
- Settings cards for theme and sound preview.

The `@expo/vector-icons` dependency is being used only for tab-bar icons in the code AI Studio generated:

```tsx
import { Ionicons } from '@expo/vector-icons';
```

So this is a small and normal dependency fix, not an architectural problem.

Do not try to install Linux system packages or fix this error:


That message comes from React Native DevTools attempting to run inside the Codespaces container. Your native app can still bundle and load in Expo Go; Metro started successfully and the tunnel connected before it reached the icon-import error.

# @GitHub done now.

You completed the first true-native milestone correctly. I verified the new commit `eb17c9e`: it adds native React Navigation bottom tabs, a React Native `SafeAreaProvider`, and three static screens—Alarm, Setup, and Settings—with no web/Vite code mixed into the app.[^15_1]

Your next step is **not** camera, alarms in the background, audio, or APK generation. Build the real editable alarm state first: wake time, weekdays, arm/disarm state, and local persistence.

## Native shell review

The committed native code is clean enough to continue:

- `App.tsx` uses `NavigationContainer` and `SafeAreaProvider`, which is the right root structure for the native app.[^15_1]
- `AppNavigator.tsx` contains exactly the three intended tabs: Alarm, Setup, and Settings.[^15_1]
- The screens use React Native primitives such as `View`, `Text`, `ScrollView`, `TouchableOpacity`, and `StyleSheet`; there are no HTML elements, `react-dom` imports, browser APIs, Vite imports, or web CSS files.[^15_1]
- The dark styling, amber primary action, blue weekday selection, rounded cards, and readable white text retain the visual direction of your original WakeUp prototype.[^15_1]
- Your project includes the navigation packages it needs, including `@react-navigation/native`, `@react-navigation/bottom-tabs`, `react-native-screens`, and `react-native-safe-area-context`.[^15_1]

This is now a genuine native mobile UI shell.

## Stage 2 goal

The next commit should make the Alarm tab **interactive**, but still remain deliberately small.

After Stage 2, your phone should allow you to:

```text
Change wake time
Select and unselect weekdays
Arm the alarm
Cancel the alarm
Keep the chosen settings after closing and reopening Expo Go
See the configured alarm information in Setup
```

It should **not** yet do any of these:

```text
Actually ring in the background
Schedule an Android exact alarm
Ask for notifications permission
Use the camera
Scan barcodes
Use motion sensors
Play alarm audio
Upload media
Process payments
Show ads
Build an APK
```

That keeps the next change focused and safe.

## Ask AI Studio now

Open Google AI Studio and paste this exact prompt:

```text
I have a true Expo React Native TypeScript project with this current structure:

App.tsx
src/navigation/AppNavigator.tsx
src/screens/AlarmScreen.tsx
src/screens/SetupScreen.tsx
src/screens/SettingsScreen.tsx
src/theme/colors.ts

The project already has three static native tabs:
Alarm, Setup, Settings.

Generate only Stage 2: interactive alarm settings and local persistence.

Strict rules:
- This is a React Native Expo app, not a web app.
- Do not use Vite, react-dom, HTML, div, span, button, input, CSS, localStorage, document, window, browser APIs, Tailwind web classes, or a WebView.
- Use TypeScript and React Native components only.
- Use StyleSheet, View, Text, Pressable, ScrollView, Modal, and SafeAreaView/react-native-safe-area-context.
- Preserve the current dark obsidian design, rounded cards, blue accent, and amber primary alarm button.
- Do not add camera, barcode scanning, background alarms, Android AlarmManager, notifications, audio, haptics, sensors, media uploads, ads, payments, promo codes, founder controls, or APK configuration.
- Do not modify app.json.
- Do not rewrite the navigation structure.
- Keep the code beginner-friendly and split state into sensible small files.

Functional requirements:

1. Add an AlarmSettings type with:
   - alarmHour: number
   - alarmMinute: number
   - repeatDays: number[]
   - isArmed: boolean
   - wakeItemName: string
   - wakeBarcode: string
   - movementEnabled: boolean
   - movementTarget: number

2. Add default settings:
   - 07:00
   - Monday to Friday selected
   - isArmed false
   - wake item: Bathroom Toothpaste
   - wake barcode: 012345678905
   - movement enabled true
   - movement target 15

3. Add one shared Settings Context:
   - All three tabs must use the same settings state.
   - Persist settings using @react-native-async-storage/async-storage.
   - Show a simple “Loading settings…” screen until saved settings have loaded.
   - If storage fails or contains invalid data, safely use default settings.
   - Do not use a database, authentication, cloud storage, or external API.

4. Alarm screen:
   - Show the live current time in 24-hour format.
   - Show the configured alarm time in 24-hour format.
   - Let the user change hour and minute with simple plus/minus controls.
   - Let the user toggle individual weekday chips.
   - Use Sunday=0 through Saturday=6.
   - Show “No repeat days selected” when none are selected.
   - Arm button behavior:
     - If no days are selected, disable the Arm Alarm button.
     - If not armed, button text is “Arm Alarm”.
     - If armed, button text is “Cancel Alarm”.
     - Armed status must be clearly visible.
   - Do not create actual alarm scheduling yet.

5. Setup screen:
   - Read from the shared settings context.
   - Show the actual current wake item name.
   - Show whether a barcode is configured.
   - Show whether the movement mission is enabled and the current movement target.
   - Keep these fields read-only for this stage.

6. Settings screen:
   - Read from the shared settings context.
   - Keep it mostly static.
   - Add a visible “Reset Alarm Settings” button.
   - Show a native confirmation modal before reset.
   - Reset returns all values to the stated defaults.

7. Accessibility:
   - Every Pressable must have an accessibilityLabel.
   - Weekday controls must announce selected/not selected state.
   - Use readable text labels, not icons only.

Return only these five sections:

1. Required packages
2. Exact terminal install command
3. Exact file tree
4. Full copy-paste code for every changed or new file
5. Exact command to start the app in Expo Go

Do not include extra explanations.
```


## Do not apply its answer yet

When AI Studio gives you its answer:

1. Do **not** run its install command yet.
2. Do **not** paste its files into Codespaces yet.
3. Copy its entire response here.

I will check four things before you touch the project:

- That it uses `AsyncStorage`, not web `localStorage`.
- That the React context/provider is correctly placed around navigation.
- That the weekday logic uses the intended Sunday–Saturday indexing.
- That it does not accidentally add background alarm code, browser code, excessive packages, or another fake purchase/advertising system.

After I approve it, you will apply it in Codespaces, run it with:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel
```

and open it in Expo Go on your Samsung. Then you will make the next clean commit:

```text
Add persisted native alarm settings
```

Yes—that is exactly what we are doing: **`alarmG2` is your visual/product blueprint; `alarmG-native` is the true native implementation of that same app.** You are not reinventing WakeUp from zero, but you cannot copy the old web code directly because React Native uses different UI components and device APIs.

Because this is a fresh Google AI Studio chat, it does not know anything about your older app unless you give it a compact design-and-behavior brief. A GitHub repository URL alone is not enough to assume it can inspect or understand the old project correctly.

## The two-project model

Keep this mental model:

```text
alarmG2
= The original WakeUp product blueprint
= Design reference, feature reference, wording reference
= Web code must stay unchanged

alarmG-native
= The new true Android-native app
= React Native + Expo implementation
= Recreates the same product gradually
```

We are carrying forward:

- The dark premium alarm-app look.
- The large clock-first Alarm screen.
- The physical wake-up barcode mission.
- The movement mission.
- The 10-digit snooze PIN concept.
- The ringing and snoozed full-screen states.
- The One UI-inspired bottom navigation.
- The theme system.
- The premium/monetization idea—but without fake payment, fake ads, or fake unlocks.

We are **not** carrying forward:

```text
Vite
react-dom
HTML elements
Tailwind web classes
```

## WakeUp Native Design Brief

Copy this entire brief and save it somewhere easy to reuse—Google Keep, a Notes app, a document, or a pinned AI Studio message.

```text
PROJECT CONTEXT — WAKEUP NATIVE

I am rebuilding an existing alarm app called WakeUp as a true native Android app using React Native + Expo.

The old project is called alarmG2. It is a web prototype and visual/product reference only.
The new project is called alarmG-native. It is the real native React Native app.

Do not copy web code from the old app.
Do not use Vite, react-dom, HTML, CSS files, browser APIs, localStorage, document, window, or WebView.
Translate the product design and behavior into proper React Native + Expo code.

PRODUCT PURPOSE

WakeUp is a friction-based morning alarm app.

The core idea:
A user should not be able to dismiss the alarm mindlessly.
They must complete a real wake-up mission, such as walking, completing a movement target, and scanning a registered physical household barcode.

Examples of wake-up items:
- Bathroom toothpaste
- Coffee bag
- Shampoo bottle
- Kitchen item
- Any registered barcode item placed away from the bed

The product should feel motivating, focused, premium, dark, calm before sleep, and urgent but readable when the alarm rings.

VISUAL STYLE

Platform:
- Android first
- Samsung / One UI inspired
- Portrait orientation
- Native mobile interface only

Core palette:
- Main background: near-black obsidian
- Cards: charcoal/slate dark surfaces
- Main normal interaction accent: blue
- Primary Arm Alarm / critical wake-up action: warm amber or golden orange
- Positive/success state: emerald green
- Error state: rose/red
- Main text: white or near-white
- Secondary text: cool muted gray

Visual principles:
- Rounded dark cards, approximately 20–24px radius
- Spacious vertical layout
- Large, highly readable digital clock
- Strong hierarchy: time first, alarm action second, supporting settings third
- Avoid clutter and excessive menus
- Use real icons plus text labels, not icon-only core actions
- Touch targets should feel generous and easy on a Samsung phone
- Use safe area spacing for status bar and gesture navigation
- Keep important actions reachable in the lower thumb zone

MAIN NAVIGATION

Use exactly three bottom tabs:

1. Alarm
2. Setup
3. Settings

Alarm tab:
- The main home screen
- Large live 24-hour clock at the top
- Shows configured wake time
- Shows selected weekdays
- Shows an armed or standby state
- Has a large amber button:
  - “Arm Alarm” when not armed
  - “Cancel Alarm” when armed
- When armed, Setup and Settings will eventually be locked to reduce sleepy sabotage
- Empty weekday selection must show:
  “No repeat days selected”
- The Arm Alarm button must be disabled when no weekdays are selected

Setup tab:
- Explains and configures the wake-up mission
- Wake-up item section
- Barcode mission section
- Movement mission section
- Eventually lets a user register a physical barcode using the phone camera
- Eventually lets a user enable/disable movement and choose a movement target
- For early native stages, it can show the actual shared values as read-only information

Settings tab:
- Dark settings cards
- Theme selection eventually includes:
  - Minimal / Obsidian: default dark blue-black theme
  - Amber: warm dark amber/orange theme
  - Neon: cyan and violet futuristic theme
  - Blossom: soft pink/purple theme
  - Gothic: dark crimson/black theme
- Sound selection will come later
- Wallpaper and custom audio will come later
- Settings should include a clear “Reset Alarm Settings” action with confirmation
- No real purchases, payments, ads, promo codes, founder controls, or developer controls yet

ALARM-RINGING EXPERIENCE — LATER STAGE

When an alarm triggers:
- Open a full-screen dark ringing experience
- Show large current time
- Show an urgent but clean “Alarm Active” state
- Use amber as the primary wake-up/action color
- The user must complete the configured movement goal before barcode scanning becomes available
- Then the user scans the registered physical barcode to dismiss
- Snooze should require a 10-digit PIN
- The physical mission must not be easily bypassed
- Manual barcode input should exist only as an explicitly labelled accessibility/camera-failure fallback
- Normal users must not have hidden override buttons

SNOOZED EXPERIENCE — LATER STAGE

- Full-screen blue/dark countdown
- Show next ring time/countdown
- Allow early scanner-based disarm
- After snooze ends, return to alarm attempt two
- After the second unattended ringing attempt expires, show a missed-alarm state

TRUTHFUL MONETIZATION RULES

I want to eventually earn money through a Lifetime Pass and optional premium features.
However:
- Never create fake payment forms
- Never collect card data directly
- Never claim Google Pay, Samsung Pay, Apple Pay, AdMob, encryption, subscriptions, or purchase restore exists unless it is actually integrated
- Never auto-set isProUser from a fake UI action
- Never include fake customer records, promo codes, founder bypasses, or fake purchase ledgers in release UI
- Before real billing exists, use honest text such as:
  “Lifetime Pass Coming Soon”
- Core alarm reliability, physical wake-up flow, and safety must remain trustworthy

DEVELOPMENT APPROACH

Work in small stages.
Do not redesign the app from scratch.
Do not add unrelated packages or features.
Preserve this product identity while implementing each requested stage with native React Native code.
```

codeTsx
import React, { createContext, useState, useEffect, useContext, ReactNode } from 'react';
import { View, Text, StyleSheet } from 'react-native';
import AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';

export type AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: number;
alarmMinute: number;
repeatDays: number[];
isArmed: boolean;
wakeItemName: string;
wakeBarcode: string;
movementEnabled: boolean;
movementTarget: number;
};

const DEFAULT_SETTINGS: AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: 7,
alarmMinute: 0,
repeatDays: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5],
isArmed: false,
wakeItemName: 'Bathroom Toothpaste',
wakeBarcode: '012345678905',
movementEnabled: true,
movementTarget: 15,
};

type SettingsContextType = {
settings: AlarmSettings;
updateSettings: (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => void;
resetSettings: () => void;
};

const SettingsContext = createContext\<SettingsContextType | undefined>(undefined);

export function SettingsProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
const [settings, setSettings] = useState\<AlarmSettings>(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true);

useEffect(() => {
loadSettings();
}, []);

const loadSettings = async () => {
try {
const storedSettings = await AsyncStorage.getItem('@wakeup_settings');
if (storedSettings !== null) {
setSettings({ ...DEFAULT_SETTINGS, ...JSON.parse(storedSettings) });
}
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to load settings', error);
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
} finally {
setIsLoading(false);
}
};

const updateSettings = async (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => {
try {
const updated = { ...settings, ...newSettings };
setSettings(updated);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(updated));
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to save settings', error);
}
};

const resetSettings = async () => {
try {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(DEFAULT_SETTINGS));
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to reset settings', error);
}
};

if (isLoading) {
return (
\<View style={styles.loadingContainer}>
\<Text style={styles.loadingText}>Loading settings...\</Text>
\</View>
);
}

return (
\<SettingsContext.Provider value={{ settings, updateSettings, resetSettings }}>
{children}
\</SettingsContext.Provider>
);
}

export function useSettings() {
const context = useContext(SettingsContext);
if (context === undefined) {
throw new Error('useSettings must be used within a SettingsProvider');
}
return context;
}

const styles = StyleSheet.create({
loadingContainer: {
flex: 1,
backgroundColor: colors.background,
alignItems: 'center',
justifyContent: 'center',
},
loadingText: {
color: colors.textSecondary,
fontSize: 16,
},
});

import React, { useState, useEffect } from 'react';
import { View, Text, StyleSheet, Pressable, ScrollView } from 'react-native';
import { useSafeAreaInsets } from 'react-native-safe-area-context';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';
import { useSettings } from '../context/SettingsContext';

const DAYS = ['S', 'M', 'T', 'W', 'T', 'F', 'S'];
const FULL_DAY_NAMES = ['Sunday', 'Monday', 'Tuesday', 'Wednesday', 'Thursday', 'Friday', 'Saturday'];

export default function AlarmScreen() {
const insets = useSafeAreaInsets();
const { settings, updateSettings } = useSettings();
const [currentTime, setCurrentTime] = useState(new Date());

useEffect(() => {
const timer = setInterval(() => {
setCurrentTime(new Date());
}, 1000);
return () => clearInterval(timer);
}, []);

const formatLiveTime = (date: Date) => {
const hh = String(date.getHours()).padStart(2, '0');
const mm = String(date.getMinutes()).padStart(2, '0');
return `${hh}:${mm}`;
};

const toggleDay = (dayIndex: number) => {
if (settings.isArmed) return;
const isSelected = settings.repeatDays.includes(dayIndex);
const newDays = isSelected
? settings.repeatDays.filter(d => d !== dayIndex)
: [...settings.repeatDays, dayIndex].sort();
updateSettings({ repeatDays: newDays });
};

const adjustHour = (delta: number) => {
if (settings.isArmed) return;
let newHour = settings.alarmHour + delta;
if (newHour > 23) newHour = 0;
if (newHour < 0) newHour = 23;
updateSettings({ alarmHour: newHour });
};

const adjustMinute = (delta: number) => {
if (settings.isArmed) return;
let newMinute = settings.alarmMinute + delta;
if (newMinute > 59) newMinute = 0;
if (newMinute < 0) newMinute = 59;
updateSettings({ alarmMinute: newMinute });
};

const toggleArmed = () => {
if (settings.repeatDays.length === 0 \&\& !settings.isArmed) return;
updateSettings({ isArmed: !settings.isArmed });
};

const noDaysSelected = settings.repeatDays.length === 0;

return (
\<ScrollView contentContainerStyle={[styles.container, { paddingTop: insets.top }]}>
\<View style={styles.clockContainer}>
\<Text style={styles.clockText}>
{formatLiveTime(currentTime)}
\</Text>
\</View>

```
  <View style={styles.card}>
    <Text style={styles.cardTitle}>Wake-up Time</Text>
    <Text style={styles.cardSubtitle}>
      {settings.isArmed ? 'Alarm is armed and locked' : 'Adjust your wake-up time'}
    </Text>

    <View style={styles.timeControlsContainer}>
      <View style={styles.timeSection}>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustHour(-1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Decrease hour"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>-</Text>
        </Pressable>
        <Text style={styles.alarmTimeText}>{String(settings.alarmHour).padStart(2, '0')}</Text>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustHour(1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Increase hour"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>+</Text>
        </Pressable>
      </View>
      
      <Text style={styles.timeColon}>:</Text>

      <View style={styles.timeSection}>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustMinute(-1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Decrease minute"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>-</Text>
        </Pressable>
        <Text style={styles.alarmTimeText}>{String(settings.alarmMinute).padStart(2, '0')}</Text>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustMinute(1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Increase minute"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>+</Text>
        </Pressable>
      </View>
    </View>

    <View style={styles.chipContainer}>
      {DAYS.map((day, index) => {
        const isSelected = settings.repeatDays.includes(index);
        return (
          <Pressable
            key={index}
            style={[
              styles.chip,
              isSelected && styles.chipActive,
              settings.isArmed && styles.chipDisabled
            ]}
            onPress={() => toggleDay(index)}
            accessibilityLabel={`Toggle ${FULL_DAY_NAMES[index]}`}
            accessibilityState={{ selected: isSelected }}
            disabled={settings.isArmed}
          >
            <Text style={[styles.chipText, isSelected && styles.chipTextActive]}>
              {day}
            </Text>
          </Pressable>
        );
      })}
    </View>
    
    {noDaysSelected && (
      <Text style={styles.warningText}>No repeat days selected</Text>
    )}
  </View>

  <View style={styles.spacer} />

  <Pressable
    style={[
      styles.primaryButton,
      noDaysSelected && !settings.isArmed ? styles.primaryButtonDisabled : null,
      settings.isArmed ? styles.primaryButtonCancel : null
    ]}
    onPress={toggleArmed}
    disabled={noDaysSelected && !settings.isArmed}
    accessibilityLabel={settings.isArmed ? "Cancel Alarm" : "Arm Alarm"}
  >
    <Text style={styles.primaryButtonText}>
      {settings.isArmed ? 'Cancel Alarm' : 'Arm Alarm'}
    </Text>
  </Pressable>
</ScrollView>
```

This is a good Stage 2 response from AI Studio: it stayed native, used `AsyncStorage` rather than browser storage, added one shared settings context, and kept the feature scope controlled. Do **not** paste it yet, because there are two small but important correctness fixes needed first: invalid stored data is not actually validated, and minute changes should carry into the hour rather than unexpectedly wrapping from `07:59` to `07:00`.

## What is correct

AI Studio followed the architecture rules well:

- It uses:

```tsx
AsyncStorage
```

rather than:

```ts
localStorage
```

- It puts a shared `SettingsProvider` around your navigation tree:

```tsx
<SafeAreaProvider>
  <SettingsProvider>
    <NavigationContainer theme={DarkTheme}>
      <AppNavigator />
    </NavigationContainer>
  </SettingsProvider>
</SafeAreaProvider>
```

- It keeps all three tabs connected to the same settings state.
- It adds a proper loading state while saved settings are being retrieved.
- It makes weekday selection interactive.
- It blocks arming when the user selects no days.
- It locks weekday/time changes when armed.
- It adds an honest reset confirmation modal.
- It does not introduce browser code, camera code, background alarms, audio, payments, ads, fake premium unlocks, or APK configuration.

That is exactly the right size for this stage.

## Fix before applying

### 1. Stored-data validation is incomplete

The AI Studio code currently does this:

```tsx
setSettings({ ...DEFAULT_SETTINGS, ...JSON.parse(storedSettings) });
```

If the saved value is malformed but still valid JSON—for example:

```json
{
  "alarmHour": "banana",
  "repeatDays": "weekday"
}
```

the app will accept it and may behave strangely or crash later.

Your requirement was:

```text
If storage fails or contains invalid data, safely use default settings.
```

The code handles a JSON parsing failure, but it does **not** validate the shape and values of parsed data.

### 2. Minute adjustment has surprising behavior

Current behavior:

```tsx
let newMinute = settings.alarmMinute + delta;

if (newMinute > 59) newMinute = 0;
if (newMinute < 0) newMinute = 59;

updateSettings({ alarmMinute: newMinute });
```

That means:

```text
07:59 + 1 minute = 07:00
07:00 - 1 minute = 07:59
```

For an alarm app, users will naturally expect:

```text
07:59 + 1 minute = 08:00
07:00 - 1 minute = 06:59
```


### 3. Use the tunnel start command

AI Studio’s command:

```bash
npx expo start --clear
```

is fine on a local computer using the same Wi-Fi network, but you are working in GitHub Codespaces. Use this instead:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel --clear
```

The `--clear` part resets Metro’s cache; the `--tunnel` part gives your Samsung phone a QR-accessible Expo connection.

```text
2. src/screens/AlarmScreen.tsx

Required correction 1 — validate loaded AsyncStorage data

The current loading code merges JSON.parse(storedSettings) directly into DEFAULT_SETTINGS.

Add a small type guard function named isValidSettings.

It must return true only when all these conditions are true:

- alarmHour is a number from 0 through 23
- alarmMinute is a number from 0 through 59
- repeatDays is an array of numbers
- every repeatDays value is an integer from 0 through 6
- isArmed is a boolean
- wakeItemName is a string
- wakeBarcode is a string
- movementEnabled is a boolean
- movementTarget is a positive number

When loading saved settings:

- If isValidSettings(parsed) is true, merge it with DEFAULT_SETTINGS.
- If parsing fails or validation fails, use DEFAULT_SETTINGS.
- Do not crash.

Required correction 2 — make minute changes carry hour changes

Update adjustMinute in AlarmScreen so:

- 23:59 plus 1 minute becomes 00:00
- 07:00 minus 1 minute becomes 06:59
- 00:00 minus 1 minute becomes 23:59

Keep the alarm hour and minute locked when isArmed is true.

Required correction 3 — improve disabled button feedback

When the Arm Alarm button is disabled because no weekdays are selected:
```

# @GitHub 1. Full final code for src/context/SettingsContext.tsx

codeTsx
import React, { createContext, useState, useEffect, useContext, ReactNode } from 'react';
import { View, Text, StyleSheet } from 'react-native';
import AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';

export type AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: number;
alarmMinute: number;
repeatDays: number[];
isArmed: boolean;
wakeItemName: string;
wakeBarcode: string;
movementEnabled: boolean;
movementTarget: number;
};

const DEFAULT_SETTINGS: AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: 7,
alarmMinute: 0,
repeatDays: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5],
isArmed: false,
wakeItemName: 'Bathroom Toothpaste',
wakeBarcode: '012345678905',
movementEnabled: true,
movementTarget: 15,
};

function isValidSettings(data: any): data is Partial\<AlarmSettings> {
if (!data || typeof data !== 'object') return false;

if ('alarmHour' in data) {
if (typeof data.alarmHour !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(data.alarmHour) || data.alarmHour < 0 || data.alarmHour > 23) return false;
}
if ('alarmMinute' in data) {
if (typeof data.alarmMinute !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(data.alarmMinute) || data.alarmMinute < 0 || data.alarmMinute > 59) return false;
}
if ('repeatDays' in data) {
if (!Array.isArray(data.repeatDays)) return false;
for (const day of data.repeatDays) {
if (typeof day !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(day) || day < 0 || day > 6) return false;
}
}
if ('isArmed' in data \&\& typeof data.isArmed !== 'boolean') return false;
if ('wakeItemName' in data \&\& typeof data.wakeItemName !== 'string') return false;
if ('wakeBarcode' in data \&\& typeof data.wakeBarcode !== 'string') return false;
if ('movementEnabled' in data \&\& typeof data.movementEnabled !== 'boolean') return false;
if ('movementTarget' in data) {
if (typeof data.movementTarget !== 'number' || data.movementTarget \<= 0) return false;
}

return true;
}

type SettingsContextType = {
settings: AlarmSettings;
updateSettings: (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => void;
resetSettings: () => void;
};

const SettingsContext = createContext\<SettingsContextType | undefined>(undefined);

export function SettingsProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
const [settings, setSettings] = useState\<AlarmSettings>(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true);

useEffect(() => {
loadSettings();
}, []);

const loadSettings = async () => {
try {
const storedSettings = await AsyncStorage.getItem('@wakeup_settings');
if (storedSettings !== null) {
const parsed = JSON.parse(storedSettings);
if (isValidSettings(parsed)) {
setSettings({ ...DEFAULT_SETTINGS, ...parsed });
} else {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
}
}
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to load settings', error);
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
} finally {
setIsLoading(false);
}
};

const updateSettings = async (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => {
try {
const updated = { ...settings, ...newSettings };
setSettings(updated);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(updated));
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to save settings', error);
}
};

const resetSettings = async () => {
try {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(DEFAULT_SETTINGS));
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to reset settings', error);
}
};

if (isLoading) {
return (
\<View style={styles.loadingContainer}>
\<Text style={styles.loadingText}>Loading settings...\</Text>
\</View>
);
}

return (
\<SettingsContext.Provider value={{ settings, updateSettings, resetSettings }}>
{children}
\</SettingsContext.Provider>
);
}

export function useSettings() {
const context = useContext(SettingsContext);
if (context === undefined) {
throw new Error('useSettings must be used within a SettingsProvider');
}
return context;
}

const styles = StyleSheet.create({
loadingContainer: {
flex: 1,
backgroundColor: colors.background,
alignItems: 'center',
justifyContent: 'center',
},
loadingText: {
color: colors.textSecondary,
fontSize: 16,
},
});

import React, { useState, useEffect } from 'react';
import { View, Text, StyleSheet, Pressable, ScrollView } from 'react-native';
import { useSafeAreaInsets } from 'react-native-safe-area-context';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';
import { useSettings } from '../context/SettingsContext';

const DAYS = ['S', 'M', 'T', 'W', 'T', 'F', 'S'];
const FULL_DAY_NAMES = ['Sunday', 'Monday', 'Tuesday', 'Wednesday', 'Thursday', 'Friday', 'Saturday'];

export default function AlarmScreen() {
const insets = useSafeAreaInsets();
const { settings, updateSettings } = useSettings();
const [currentTime, setCurrentTime] = useState(new Date());

useEffect(() => {
const timer = setInterval(() => {
setCurrentTime(new Date());
}, 1000);
return () => clearInterval(timer);
}, []);

const formatLiveTime = (date: Date) => {
const hh = String(date.getHours()).padStart(2, '0');
const mm = String(date.getMinutes()).padStart(2, '0');
return `${hh}:${mm}`;
};

const toggleDay = (dayIndex: number) => {
if (settings.isArmed) return;
const isSelected = settings.repeatDays.includes(dayIndex);
const newDays = isSelected
? settings.repeatDays.filter(d => d !== dayIndex)
: [...settings.repeatDays, dayIndex].sort();
updateSettings({ repeatDays: newDays });
};

const adjustHour = (delta: number) => {
if (settings.isArmed) return;
let newHour = settings.alarmHour + delta;
if (newHour > 23) newHour = 0;
if (newHour < 0) newHour = 23;
updateSettings({ alarmHour: newHour });
};

const adjustMinute = (delta: number) => {
if (settings.isArmed) return;

```
let newMinute = settings.alarmMinute + delta;
let newHour = settings.alarmHour;

if (newMinute > 59) {
  newMinute = 0;
  newHour += 1;
  if (newHour > 23) newHour = 0;
} else if (newMinute < 0) {
  newMinute = 59;
  newHour -= 1;
  if (newHour < 0) newHour = 23;
}

updateSettings({ alarmHour: newHour, alarmMinute: newMinute });
```

};

const toggleArmed = () => {
if (settings.repeatDays.length === 0 \&\& !settings.isArmed) return;
updateSettings({ isArmed: !settings.isArmed });
};

const noDaysSelected = settings.repeatDays.length === 0;
const isArmButtonDisabled = noDaysSelected \&\& !settings.isArmed;

return (
\<ScrollView contentContainerStyle={[styles.container, { paddingTop: insets.top }]}>
\<View style={styles.clockContainer}>
\<Text style={styles.clockText}>
{formatLiveTime(currentTime)}
\</Text>
\</View>

```
  <View style={styles.card}>
    <Text style={styles.cardTitle}>Wake-up Time</Text>
    <Text style={styles.cardSubtitle}>
      {settings.isArmed ? 'Alarm is armed and locked' : 'Adjust your wake-up time'}
    </Text>

    <View style={styles.timeControlsContainer}>
      <View style={styles.timeSection}>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustHour(-1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Decrease hour"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>-</Text>
        </Pressable>
        <Text style={styles.alarmTimeText}>{String(settings.alarmHour).padStart(2, '0')}</Text>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustHour(1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Increase hour"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>+</Text>
        </Pressable>
      </View>
      
      <Text style={styles.timeColon}>:</Text>

      <View style={styles.timeSection}>
        <Pressable
          style={styles.adjustButton}
          onPress={() => adjustMinute(-1)}
          accessibilityLabel="Decrease minute"
          disabled={settings.isArmed}
        >
          <Text style={styles.adjustButtonText}>-</Text>
        </Pressable>
        <Text style={styles.alarmTimeText}>{String(settings.alarmMinute).padStart(2, '0')}</Text>
        <Pressable

    <View style={styles.chipContainer}>
      {DAYS.map((day, index) => {

            key={index}

              {day}
            </Text>
          </Pressable>
        );
      })}
    </View>
    
    {noDaysSelected && (
      <Text style={styles.warningText}>No repeat days selected</Text>
    )}
  </View>

  <View style={styles.spacer} />

  <Pressable
    style={[
      styles.primaryButton,
      isArmButtonDisabled ? styles.primaryButtonDisabled : null,

    ]}
    onPress={toggleArmed}

    accessibilityLabel={settings.isArmed ? "Cancel Alarm" : "Arm Alarm"}
    accessibilityHint={isArmButtonDisabled ? "Select at least one repeat day before arming the alarm." : undefined}
  >
    <Text style={styles.primaryButtonText}>
      {settings.isArmed ? 'Cancel Alarm' : 'Arm Alarm'}
    </Text>
  </Pressable>
</ScrollView>
```

## What is already good

The revised `AlarmScreen.tsx` is correct in the visible interaction behavior:

- It uses React Native only.
- The live clock is 24-hour format.
- Weekday values correctly use Sunday `0` through Saturday `6`.
- Empty weekday selection shows:

```text
No repeat days selected
```

- The Arm Alarm button is disabled when no days are selected.
- Minute changes now correctly carry to the hour:

```text
07:59 + 1 minute = 08:00
00:00 - 1 minute = 23:59
```

- The time/weekday controls lock while armed.
- Accessibility labels and the disabled-arm-button hint are present.

The AsyncStorage package and the tunnel command are also correct:

```bash
npx expo install @react-native-async-storage/async-storage
```

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel --clear
```


## Two fixes needed

### 1. Rapid updates can overwrite each other

The current `updateSettings` function uses the `settings` value captured when the screen rendered:

```tsx
const updated = { ...settings, ...newSettings };
setSettings(updated);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(updated));
```

Imagine the user quickly taps Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday:

1. First tap calculates a new list based on old settings.
2. Before React re-renders, second tap can still use that same old settings object.
3. One of the selected days can be lost.

The fix is to use React’s functional state update:

```tsx
setSettings((previousSettings) => {
  const updatedSettings = {
    ...previousSettings,
    ...newSettings,
  };

  return updatedSettings;
});
```


### 2. `isArmed` must not survive restart yet

At this stage, your app has no native Android alarm scheduler. So if someone arms the visual UI, closes Expo Go, and reopens it, the app must not restore:

```ts
isArmed: true
```

Otherwise it tells the user “Alarm is armed and locked,” but there is no real alarm scheduled behind it.

For now:

```text
Persist wake time and selected weekdays.
Do not restore the armed state after app restart.
```

Later, when Android `AlarmManager` is genuinely integrated, native scheduling will become the authority for armed/unarmed state.

```text
Do not add new packages.
Do not add alarms, notifications, camera, barcode scanning, audio, sensors, payments, ads, native Android code, or browser APIs.

Make these two exact corrections:

1. Fix updateSettings so rapid changes cannot overwrite each other.

The current implementation creates updated settings from the stale outer settings value.

Replace updateSettings with a functional React state update that uses:

setSettings((previousSettings) => {
  const updatedSettings = {
    ...previousSettings,
    ...newSettings,
  };

  // persist updatedSettings safely
  return updatedSettings;
});

Persist the same updatedSettings object to AsyncStorage.
Keep the existing console error handling.
Do not use localStorage.

2. Do not restore armed state after reopening the app.

In loadSettings:
- If stored settings are valid, merge them with DEFAULT_SETTINGS.
- Force isArmed to false after loading.
- Add this comment directly above the forced value:

// Native alarm scheduling will own armed state in a later stage.

The resulting load behavior must be equivalent to:

setSettings({
  ...DEFAULT_SETTINGS,
  ...parsed,
  // Native alarm scheduling will own armed state in a later stage.
  isArmed: false,
});

3. Improve validation slightly.

The isValidSettings function may continue accepting a partial saved settings object because defaults are merged afterward.

However:
- If alarmHour exists, it must be an integer from 0 to 23.
- If alarmMinute exists, it must be an integer from 0 to 59.
- If repeatDays exists, it must be an array of integer values from 0 to 6.
- If movementTarget exists, it must be a finite positive integer.
- Existing type checks for boolean/string values must remain.

Return only the full final code for:

src/context/SettingsContext.tsx
```

# @GitHub import React, { createContext, useState, useEffect, useContext, ReactNode } from 'react';

import { View, Text, StyleSheet } from 'react-native';
import AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';

export type AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: number;
alarmMinute: number;
repeatDays: number[];
isArmed: boolean;
wakeItemName: string;
wakeBarcode: string;
movementEnabled: boolean;
movementTarget: number;
};

const DEFAULT_SETTINGS: AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: 7,
alarmMinute: 0,
repeatDays: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5],
isArmed: false,
wakeItemName: 'Bathroom Toothpaste',
wakeBarcode: '012345678905',
movementEnabled: true,
movementTarget: 15,
};

function isValidSettings(data: any): data is Partial\<AlarmSettings> {
if (!data || typeof data !== 'object') return false;

if ('alarmHour' in data) {
if (typeof data.alarmHour !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(data.alarmHour) || data.alarmHour < 0 || data.alarmHour > 23) return false;
}
if ('alarmMinute' in data) {
if (typeof data.alarmMinute !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(data.alarmMinute) || data.alarmMinute < 0 || data.alarmMinute > 59) return false;
}
if ('repeatDays' in data) {
if (!Array.isArray(data.repeatDays)) return false;
for (const day of data.repeatDays) {
if (typeof day !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(day) || day < 0 || day > 6) return false;
}
}
if ('isArmed' in data \&\& typeof data.isArmed !== 'boolean') return false;
if ('wakeItemName' in data \&\& typeof data.wakeItemName !== 'string') return false;
if ('wakeBarcode' in data \&\& typeof data.wakeBarcode !== 'string') return false;
if ('movementEnabled' in data \&\& typeof data.movementEnabled !== 'boolean') return false;
if ('movementTarget' in data) {
if (typeof data.movementTarget !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(data.movementTarget) || !Number.isInteger(data.movementTarget) || data.movementTarget \<= 0) return false;
}

return true;
}

type SettingsContextType = {
settings: AlarmSettings;
updateSettings: (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => void;
resetSettings: () => void;
};

const SettingsContext = createContext\<SettingsContextType | undefined>(undefined);

export function SettingsProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
const [settings, setSettings] = useState\<AlarmSettings>(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true);

useEffect(() => {
loadSettings();
}, []);

const loadSettings = async () => {
try {
const storedSettings = await AsyncStorage.getItem('@wakeup_settings');
if (storedSettings !== null) {
const parsed = JSON.parse(storedSettings);
if (isValidSettings(parsed)) {
setSettings({
...DEFAULT_SETTINGS,
...parsed,
// Native alarm scheduling will own armed state in a later stage.
isArmed: false,
});
} else {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
}
}
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to load settings', error);
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
} finally {
setIsLoading(false);
}
};

const updateSettings = (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => {
setSettings((previousSettings) => {
const updatedSettings = {
...previousSettings,
...newSettings,
};

```
  // persist updatedSettings safely
  AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(updatedSettings))
    .catch(error => console.error('Failed to save settings', error));

  return updatedSettings;
});
```

};

const resetSettings = async () => {
try {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(DEFAULT_SETTINGS));
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to reset settings', error);
}
};

if (isLoading) {
return (
\<View style={styles.loadingContainer}>
\<Text style={styles.loadingText}>Loading settings...\</Text>
\</View>
);
}

return (
\<SettingsContext.Provider value={{ settings, updateSettings, resetSettings }}>
{children}
\</SettingsContext.Provider>
);
}

export function useSettings() {
const context = useContext(SettingsContext);
if (context === undefined) {
throw new Error('useSettings must be used within a SettingsProvider');
}
return context;
}

const styles = StyleSheet.create({
loadingContainer: {
flex: 1,
backgroundColor: colors.background,
alignItems: 'center',
justifyContent: 'center',
},
loadingText: {
color: colors.textSecondary,
fontSize: 16,
},
});

Approved. This final `SettingsContext.tsx` is suitable for Stage 2: it validates stored values, uses functional state updates so rapid taps do not lose settings, preserves the actual alarm configuration, and deliberately restores `isArmed` as `false` until native Android scheduling exists.

## Open it on Samsung

Run this exact command:

```bash
npx expo start --tunnel --clear
```

Then:

1. Open **Expo Go** on your Samsung.
2. Scan the QR code.
3. Open the native WakeUp app.

The app should now behave like this:


| Area | Expected behavior |
| :-- | :-- |
| Alarm tab | Shows the live time in 24-hour format |
| Hour controls | `+` and `−` change the configured hour |
| Minute controls | `07:59` + one minute becomes `08:00`; `00:00` − one minute becomes `23:59` |
| Weekdays | Each day can be selected/unselected |
| No selected days | Shows “No repeat days selected” and disables Arm Alarm |
| Arm button | Changes between “Arm Alarm” and “Cancel Alarm” |
| Armed state | Disables time and weekday controls |
| Setup tab | Reflects the real shared wake item, barcode status, movement target, and movement enabled state |
| Settings tab | Reset Alarm Settings opens a confirmation modal |
| Reset | Restores 07:00, weekdays, Bathroom Toothpaste, barcode, movement target 15 |
| Reopen Expo Go | Time and weekday settings remain saved; armed state deliberately returns to standby |

The armed state returning to standby after reopening is **correct at this stage**. It prevents the app from pretending that a real Android alarm is scheduled before we build the native alarm scheduler.

# @GitHub import React, { createContext, useState, useEffect, useContext, ReactNode } from 'react';

import { View, Text, StyleSheet } from 'react-native';
import AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';

export type AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: number;
alarmMinute: number;
repeatDays: number[];
isArmed: boolean;
wakeItemName: string;
wakeBarcode: string;
movementEnabled: boolean;
movementTarget: number;
};

const DEFAULT_SETTINGS: AlarmSettings = {
alarmHour: 7,
alarmMinute: 0,
repeatDays: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5],
isArmed: false,
wakeItemName: 'Bathroom Toothpaste',
wakeBarcode: '012345678905',
movementEnabled: true,
movementTarget: 15,
};

function isValidSettings(data: any): data is Partial\<AlarmSettings> {
if (!data || typeof data !== 'object') return false;

if ('alarmHour' in data) {
if (typeof data.alarmHour !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(data.alarmHour) || data.alarmHour < 0 || data.alarmHour > 23) return false;
}
if ('alarmMinute' in data) {
if (typeof data.alarmMinute !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(data.alarmMinute) || data.alarmMinute < 0 || data.alarmMinute > 59) return false;
}
if ('repeatDays' in data) {
if (!Array.isArray(data.repeatDays)) return false;
for (const day of data.repeatDays) {
if (typeof day !== 'number' || !Number.isInteger(day) || day < 0 || day > 6) return false;
}
}
if ('isArmed' in data \&\& typeof data.isArmed !== 'boolean') return false;
if ('wakeItemName' in data \&\& typeof data.wakeItemName !== 'string') return false;
if ('wakeBarcode' in data \&\& typeof data.wakeBarcode !== 'string') return false;
if ('movementEnabled' in data \&\& typeof data.movementEnabled !== 'boolean') return false;
if ('movementTarget' in data) {
if (typeof data.movementTarget !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(data.movementTarget) || !Number.isInteger(data.movementTarget) || data.movementTarget \<= 0) return false;
}

return true;
}

type SettingsContextType = {
settings: AlarmSettings;
updateSettings: (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => void;
resetSettings: () => void;
};

const SettingsContext = createContext\<SettingsContextType | undefined>(undefined);

export function SettingsProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
const [settings, setSettings] = useState\<AlarmSettings>(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState(true);

useEffect(() => {
loadSettings();
}, []);

const loadSettings = async () => {
try {
const storedSettings = await AsyncStorage.getItem('@wakeup_settings');
if (storedSettings !== null) {
const parsed = JSON.parse(storedSettings);
if (isValidSettings(parsed)) {
setSettings({
...DEFAULT_SETTINGS,
...parsed,
// Native alarm scheduling will own armed state in a later stage.
isArmed: false,
});
} else {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
}
}
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to load settings', error);
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
} finally {
setIsLoading(false);
}
};

const updateSettings = (newSettings: Partial\<AlarmSettings>) => {
setSettings((previousSettings) => {
const updatedSettings = {
...previousSettings,
...newSettings,
};

// persist updatedSettings safely
AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(updatedSettings))
.catch(error => console.error('Failed to save settings', error));

return updatedSettings;
});
};

const resetSettings = async () => {
try {
setSettings(DEFAULT_SETTINGS);
await AsyncStorage.setItem('@wakeup_settings', JSON.stringify(DEFAULT_SETTINGS));
} catch (error) {
console.error('Failed to reset settings', error);
}
};

if (isLoading) {
return (
\<View style={styles.loadingContainer}>
\<Text style={styles.loadingText}>Loading settings...\</Text>
\</View>
);
}

return (
\<SettingsContext.Provider value={{ settings, updateSettings, resetSettings }}>
{children}
\</SettingsContext.Provider>
);
}

export function useSettings() {
const context = useContext(SettingsContext);
if (context === undefined) {
throw new Error('useSettings must be used within a SettingsProvider');
}
return context;
}

const styles = StyleSheet.create({
loadingContainer: {
flex: 1,
backgroundColor: colors.background,
alignItems: 'center',
justifyContent: 'center',
},
loadingText: {
color: colors.textSecondary,
fontSize: 16,
},
});
the able is full settings context ive pasted,
commit id is 1f2fecd
yes all thsi worked as of now Whether weekday selection, time controls, arm/cancel, reset, and saved settings worked

You have completed Stage 2 successfully. Commit `1f2fecd` is present in `alarmG-native` and includes the shared `SettingsContext`, AsyncStorage dependency, updated Alarm screen, Setup screen, Settings reset flow, and the persisted native alarm-settings milestone.[^20_1]

Your next stage is **native mission configuration and barcode registration**. This is where the Setup tab begins to resemble your original WakeUp app: the user can name their physical wake-up item, configure movement friction, and scan a real barcode with the Samsung camera.

## Stage 3 scope

After this stage, the native app should let a user:

```text
Set a wake-up item name
Scan and save a physical barcode using the Samsung camera
See saved barcode status
Turn movement mission on/off
Change movement target
Keep all those values after reopening the app
```

It should still **not** do any of these yet:

```text
Ring at a scheduled time
Run while Android is closed
Use AlarmManager
Use notifications
Play alarm sound
Use haptics
Show ringing or snoozed screens
Require a barcode to dismiss an alarm
Use payments or ads
Generate an APK
```

That keeps the project controlled and prevents hardware features from being mixed with the much harder native alarm-scheduling stage.

## Camera technology choice

For barcode scanning, use:

```text
expo-camera
```

Do **not** use:

```text
expo-barcode-scanner
```

Expo marks the old standalone barcode-scanner package as deprecated and recommends barcode scanning through `expo-camera`. `CameraView` can show a native camera preview and detect barcodes, while `expo-camera` supplies Android camera permission support.[^20_2][^20_3][^20_4]

export const colors = {
background: '\#0a0a0a',
card: '\#1a1a1a',
text: '\#ffffff',

/src/screens/SetupScreen.tsx
codeTsx
import React, { useState } from 'react';
import { View, Text, StyleSheet, ScrollView, TextInput, Pressable, Modal, Linking } from 'react-native';
import { useSafeAreaInsets } from 'react-native-safe-area-context';
import { CameraView, useCameraPermissions } from 'expo-camera';
import { colors } from '../theme/colors';
import { useSettings } from '../context/SettingsContext';

export default function SetupScreen() {
const insets = useSafeAreaInsets();
const { settings, updateSettings } = useSettings();

const [permission, requestPermission] = useCameraPermissions();
const [isScanning, setIsScanning] = useState(false);
const [scanned, setScanned] = useState(false);
const [showSuccess, setShowSuccess] = useState(false);
const [manualBarcode, setManualBarcode] = useState('');

const isBarcodeConfigured = Boolean(settings.wakeBarcode \&\& settings.wakeBarcode.length > 0);
const lastFour = isBarcodeConfigured ? settings.wakeBarcode.slice(-4) : '';

const handleOpenScanner = async () => {
setScanned(false);
if (!permission?.granted) {
const result = await requestPermission();
if (!result.granted) {
setIsScanning(true);
return;
}
}
setIsScanning(true);
};

const handleBarcodeScanned = (scanningResult: { type: string; data: string }) => {
if (scanned) return;
setScanned(true);
updateSettings({ wakeBarcode: scanningResult.data });
setIsScanning(false);
triggerSuccess();
};

const handleManualSubmit = () => {
if (!manualBarcode.trim()) return;
updateSettings({ wakeBarcode: manualBarcode.trim() });
setManualBarcode('');
setIsScanning(false);
triggerSuccess();
};

const triggerSuccess = () => {
setShowSuccess(true);
setTimeout(() => {
setShowSuccess(false);
}, 4000);
};

const adjustMovement = (delta: number) => {
let newTarget = settings.movementTarget + delta;
if (newTarget < 5) newTarget = 5;
if (newTarget > 50) newTarget = 50;
updateSettings({ movementTarget: newTarget });
};

return (
\<View style={[styles.container, { paddingTop: insets.top }]}>
\<ScrollView contentContainerStyle={styles.scrollContent}>
\<Text style={styles.header}>Mission Setup\</Text>

```
    <View style={styles.card}>
      <Text style={styles.cardTitle}>Wake-up Item</Text>
      <Text style={styles.cardDescription}>
        Name the physical item you will scan to turn off the alarm.
      </Text>
      <TextInput
        style={styles.input}
        value={settings.wakeItemName}
        onChangeText={(text) => updateSettings({ wakeItemName: text })}
        placeholder="e.g. Bathroom Toothpaste"
        placeholderTextColor={colors.textSecondary}
        accessibilityLabel="Wake-up item name"
      />
    </View>

    <View style={styles.card}>
      <Text style={styles.cardTitle}>Barcode Mission</Text>
      <Text style={styles.cardDescription}>
        Scan the barcode on your {settings.wakeItemName || 'item'} to wake up.
      </Text>

      {isBarcodeConfigured ? (
        <View style={styles.statusBox}>
          <Text style={styles.statusConfigured}>Barcode configured</Text>
          <Text style={styles.statusDetail}>Saved barcode ending in {lastFour}</Text>
        </View>
      ) : (
        <Text style={styles.statusMissing}>No barcode configured</Text>
      )}

      {showSuccess && (
        <View style={styles.successBadge}>
          <Text style={styles.successText}>Wake-up item barcode saved</Text>
        </View>
      )}

      <Pressable 
        style={styles.scanButton}
        onPress={handleOpenScanner}
        accessibilityLabel="Scan Wake-up Item Barcode"
      >
        <Text style={styles.scanButtonText}>Scan Wake-up Item Barcode</Text>
      </Pressable>
    </View>

    <View style={styles.card}>
      <Text style={styles.cardTitle}>Movement Mission</Text>
      <Text style={styles.cardDescription}>
        Walk steps away from your bed to dismiss the alarm.
      </Text>
      
      <Pressable
        style={[styles.toggleButton, settings.movementEnabled && styles.toggleButtonActive]}
        onPress={() => updateSettings({ movementEnabled: !settings.movementEnabled })}
        accessibilityLabel={`Movement mission is ${settings.movementEnabled ? 'Enabled' : 'Disabled'}. Tap to toggle.`}
      >
        <Text style={[styles.toggleText, settings.movementEnabled && styles.toggleTextActive]}>
          {settings.movementEnabled ? 'Enabled' : 'Disabled'}
        </Text>
      </Pressable>

      {settings.movementEnabled && (
        <View style={styles.targetContainer}>
          <Text style={styles.targetLabel}>Movement target: {settings.movementTarget}</Text>
          <View style={styles.targetControls}>
            <Pressable 
              style={styles.adjustBtn} 
              onPress={() => adjustMovement(-1)}
              accessibilityLabel="Decrease steps"
            >
              <Text style={styles.adjustBtnText}>-</Text>
            </Pressable>
            <Text style={styles.targetValue}>{settings.movementTarget}</Text>
            <Pressable 
              style={styles.adjustBtn} 
              onPress={() => adjustMovement(1)}
              accessibilityLabel="Increase steps"
            >
              <Text style={styles.adjustBtnText}>+</Text>
            </Pressable>
          </View>
        </View>
      )}
    </View>
  </ScrollView>

  <Modal visible={isScanning} animationType="slide" transparent={false} onRequestClose={() => setIsScanning(false)}>
    <View style={[styles.modalContainer, { paddingTop: insets.top, paddingBottom: insets.bottom }]}>
      {!permission?.granted ? (
        <View style={styles.permissionContainer}>
          <Text style={styles.permissionTitle}>Camera Permission Required</Text>
          <Text style={styles.permissionText}>Camera access is needed to scan your wake-up item.</Text>
          
          <Pressable style={styles.blueButton} onPress={() => Linking.openSettings()}>
            <Text style={styles.blueButtonText}>Open Settings</Text>
          </Pressable>

          <View style={styles.fallbackContainer}>
            <View style={styles.fallbackHeader}>
              <Text style={styles.fallbackTitle}>Accessibility fallback</Text>
              <Text style={styles.fallbackDesc}>Only for when camera access is unavailable.</Text>
            </View>
            <TextInput
              style={styles.input}
              value={manualBarcode}
              onChangeText={setManualBarcode}
              placeholder="Enter barcode number manually"
              placeholderTextColor={colors.textSecondary}
              keyboardType="numeric"
              accessibilityLabel="Manual barcode input"
            />
            <Pressable 
              style={[styles.scanButton, !manualBarcode.trim() && styles.scanButtonDisabled]} 
              onPress={handleManualSubmit}
              disabled={!manualBarcode.trim()}
            >
              <Text style={styles.scanButtonText}>Save Manual Barcode</Text>
            </Pressable>
          </View>

          <View style={styles.modalFooter}>
            <Pressable style={styles.cancelButton} onPress={() => setIsScanning(false)}>
              <Text style={styles.cancelButtonText}>Cancel</Text>
            </Pressable>
          </View>
        </View>
      ) : (
        <View style={styles.cameraWrapper}>
          <CameraView
            style={StyleSheet.absoluteFillObject}
            facing="back"
            barcodeScannerSettings={{
              barcodeTypes: ['ean13', 'ean8', 'upc_a', 'upc_e', 'code128', 'code39', 'qr'],
            }}
            onBarcodeScanned={scanned ? undefined : handleBarcodeScanned}
          >
            <View style={styles.cameraOverlay}>
              <View style={styles.cameraHeaderBox}>
                <Text style={styles.cameraHeaderText}>Scan {settings.wakeItemName}</Text>
                <Text style={styles.cameraSubHeaderText}>Position the barcode in the center</Text>
              </View>
              
              <View style={styles.cameraFooter}>
                <Pressable style={styles.cancelButton} onPress={() => setIsScanning(false)}>
                  <Text style={styles.cancelButtonText}>Cancel</Text>
                </Pressable>
              </View>
            </View>
          </CameraView>
        </View>
      )}
    </View>
  </Modal>
</View>
```

);
}
